▶ setup / trigger rules on synthetic bars (information-time)
  ✔ compression -> expansion: a 1.5 ATR directional bar closing beyond a <= 3 ATR 12-bar range triggers BUY/SELL with the range end as stop; a mid-close bar does not (0.905ms)
  ✔ expansion requires compression at t-1: a trending prior window (12-bar range >> 3 ATR) is not a setup (0.5152ms)
  ✔ liquidity sweep: a bar trading beyond a confirmed swing (known at t-1, >= 6 bars old) and closing back inside is a sweep; the swing is only known 3 bars after the pivot (2.3265ms)
  ✔ reclaim confirmation (B2) = first later close beyond the sweep bar opposite extreme; structure break (C1) = first close beyond the confirmed swing; the second consecutive close is confirmation (C2) (0.5732ms)

  ✔ pivot HIGH: empty gap (pivotRightBars=1, no bars physically exist between location and confirmation) → both false (0.1983ms)
  ✔ pivot LOW: a gap bar whose WICK (low) undercuts the pivot but whose CLOSE does not → wick-interacted=true, close-consumed=false (0.2394ms)
  ✔ pivot LOW: a gap bar whose CLOSE undercuts the pivot → both true (0.2492ms)
  ✔ a born-wick-interacted level is created with swept=true, so it can never later masquerade as a "first fresh sweep" (0.4829ms)
  ✔ a born-close-consumed level is created with consumed=true, so it can never later fake a BOS/CHoCH (0.2631ms)
✔ Born-consumed: CLOSE vs WICK, computed and wired separately (5.6501ms)
▶ Bounded active-level retention
  ✔ an older, still-unbroken high level survives a newer pivot confirming (not silently discarded) (1.5883ms)
  ✔ the older level can still generate its own legitimate break after a newer pivot has been retained alongside it (0.3714ms)
  ✔ documented supersession: capacity eviction (FIFO) removes exactly the oldest level when the cap is exceeded (0.3487ms)
  ✔ bounded level storage cannot grow without limit even under sustained pivot formation (0.3878ms)
  ✔ the mirror low-side retention behaves identically (older low survives a newer pivot low) (0.2443ms)
✔ Bounded active-level retention (3.5048ms)
▶ Deterministic level-selection priority (one authoritative event per bar per side)
  ✔ one bar closing above MULTIPLE active high levels fires exactly one event, for the MOST RECENTLY CONFIRMED level, and retires every level actually crossed (0.3088ms)
  ✔ one bar closing below MULTIPLE active low levels mirrors the same priority rule (0.2049ms)
  ✔ a level that is not actually crossed by this bar is left untouched even when a different level fires (0.1546ms)
  ✔ the same retained level cannot fire BOS/CHoCH twice — once consumed, later bars skip it entirely (0.194ms)
  ✔ the same retained level cannot fire a sweep twice (0.2272ms)
  ✔ a level can sweep once and remain eligible for a LATER genuine close-based break (documented lifecycle) (0.1541ms)

▶ P4B runtime-safety: every array.size()-bounded for-loop is guarded against an empty array
  ✔ every "for i = 0 to array.size(IDENT) - 1" is immediately preceded (skipping blank/comment lines) by "if array.size(IDENT) > 0" (3.4386ms)
  ✔ the P5 OUTCOME RESOLUTION backward loop (for safe in-loop array.remove) is guarded against an empty openSignals array exactly like every forward loop (0.6241ms)
  ✔ array.remove() now legitimately exists (P5 review/fix), but ONLY as the one safe in-loop removal inside the guarded, backward-iterating P5 resolution loop — no other unsafe indexed-collection function (array.first/array.last/array.pop/array.insert) exists anywhere (1.3736ms)
  ✔ array.shift() call sites remain safe: P2's two capacity-eviction sites remain guarded by "size > maxActiveLevels" preceded by array.push() (guaranteeing size >= 1); P5's recentHistory (closed-only) eviction is guarded by "size >= p5MaxTrackedSignals" instead (a capacity check ahead of the push, not a post-push overflow check — still provably safe, since array.shift() on an empty array is never reachable there either: the guard only ever removes an EXISTING oldest element to make room, and array.push() unconditionally follows it in the same block). Critically, openSignals (the never-evicted OPEN tracker) must have NO array.shift() call anywhere. (2.8642ms)
  ✔ the debug-table convenience reads (last-level consumed/swept) remain guarded by their own ternary size>0 check — unchanged by this patch (0.8392ms)
  ✔ f_nearestAbove and f_nearestBelow (SL/TP2 structural-anchor helpers) are also guarded (0.4922ms)
✔ P4B runtime-safety: every array.size()-bounded for-loop is guarded against an empty array (11.8476ms)
